# Archive: OPEN_ITEMS "Open rows" before the 19 October 2026 re-triage

This archive is evidence. Do not edit it.

On 19 Oct 2026 (HEAD 92ca6e6, then 65b14f5) the plan's "Open rows" section held 670 entries. Eight read-only agents triaged every one against the current corpus: **339 DONE, 222 STILL OPEN, 56 OWNER, 53 UNCLEAR.**

The section was replaced by the re-triaged list now in project/plans/OPEN_ITEMS.md. Below are (1) the full triage, with per-row evidence, and (2) the old section verbatim.

**Caveat from the triage.** DONE means the text a row describes is gone or changed. It does not mean the replacement was rules-verified. Rows whose defect may have been reworded rather than fixed are the known blind spot.

---

## (1) Triage

# OPEN_ITEMS.md "Open rows" triage — 19 Oct 2026, HEAD 92ca6e6

Read-only triage of every row and sub-item under "## Open rows" (plan lines 351–1131), done by eight parallel readers over disjoint line ranges (parts A–H below). Rulebooks were NOT consulted: DONE means the text the row describes is gone or changed, not that the replacement is correct.

**Caveat:** while this ran, 15 `content/` files were being modified in the working tree by concurrent work (language_and_glossary, on_ice_communication, rink_map, rules_primer, uk_rules, playing_without_the_puck, scanning_and_anticipation, time_and_space, center, goaltender, winger, defensive_zone_coverage, game_management, special_teams, body_contact_and_battles). Line numbers are working-tree lines at time of check and will drift.

Roughly half the section is closed and can be pruned to review records. Many "STILL OPEN" entries are corpus-wide sweeps or tools that were never run or built, not defects found at a location.

## Proposed dispatch — STILL OPEN content defects grouped by file (merged from parts A–H)

Line numbers are working-tree lines at time of check. Brief each one as a CLAIM to find in every layer, not as a line.

**Cross-file claim sweeps (one agent per claim, or fold into per-file agents):**
- "take it on your forearm and hip" board-posture wording in inherited docs (owner's KEEP/DEMOTE ruling): on_ice_communication :108, defender :144/:906/:991, switching_positions :213, zone_entries :565/:1168/:1235, conditioning_and_recovery :212/:613/:685, getting_started :490. Body_contact :33/:51/:1973 is probably the owning doc (keep). passing_and_receiving board-posture limb in every layer (:195 … KT14 :905).
- "minor/female" misheard by TTS: body_contact :739, passing :437, winger :747, center :766, zone_entries :852, defensive_zone_coverage :180.
- Stale "four books" frames (corpus holds six): body_contact :563/:618/:1644 facts (+8 framings), game_management :143/:1133/:1141/:1169, team_play_and_culture :137, switching_positions :290, breakouts :562, defender :1003 and center :924 ("Rules Primer carries that four-book split"). faceoffs five-vs-six (:4/:656 vs :1031).
- CARHA 32(d) flat one-game suspension, with no "at least": breakouts :214, offensive_zone_play :715, shooting :161/:191, playing_without_the_puck :740, defensive_zone_coverage :598. Tied to owner question at plan :582.
- Slash voiced as "or": time_and_space :499, puck_support_and_spacing :587, defending_the_rush :731.
- HC 8.5(b)/7.1(c)(iii) injure limb missing: winger KT :864, center :833/:903, risk_management :755 (cites CARHA 48(c) without HC 7.1(c)(iii)), passing_and_receiving :486 (facts/CM/KT15 drop "deliberately").
- HC 6.1(g) net-off not named: goaltender, game_management :624/:637. game_management :637/:1006 also lacks USAH 610(d).
- Missing PWHL: goaltender :795 (81.1), special_teams :685 (68.2), rules_primer :775.
- "Hockey Canada 10.1(i)" citation form (cosmetic): breakouts :650/:656, defensive_zone_coverage :177/:203, body_contact :1145/:1161.

**Per file:**
- **rules_primer.md:** :462 HC ladder states condition before scope; :1157 KT "ask which IIHF edition" has no Elite League half; :961 heading level; :895 and :1014 tables at 0 headroom or dropped from audio (no inline-voicing check recorded); speech: a run of 19 consecutive "Important.", "Fourth, If", "under ten is" read in lower case; 2 quote-drift flags.

- **goaltender.md:** :1246 charging-ejection list lacks EIHL Casebook 42.

- **passing_and_receiving.md:** no CM/KT carries the HC crease where-you-are test; :440 "7.6 itself" in a J/S line.

- **getting_started.md:** :224 "hockey 101" spoken as "one hundred and one".
- **how_to_watch_hockey.md:** :733 trailer says "local primary text" (low).

**Readability, corpus-wide:** 56 paragraphs over 1,200 chars (language_and_glossary 10, playing_without_the_puck 9, defending_the_rush 4, faceoffs 4); 147 KTs over 60 words; KT counts above 10 in 14 documents (uk_rules 23 … puck_handling 13), which needs the owner to say whether 5–10 binds; item 2 "rules last" never applied to the 19 R1-only documents; IIHF Table 16/14 in 17 documents (the edition decision is open).

**Site:** ReadingTools.astro:9 stale path comment; callout `<aside>`s lack a role (remark-corpus.mjs); global.css:951 stale anchor comment; about 24 literal `*` in `<main>` on 13 pages; AudioPlayer.astro:27 unversioned resume key; podcast.xml.ts:36 stale heading.

**sources/ (coordinator):** rename `hc_2026-08-print.txt`; refresh ihuk_nihl_roc.pdf (325,259 B on disk vs 369,646 B published); README:2010 "NOT CONFIRMED" sits under the correction that confirmed it; README lacks a note on the HC 8.5(b) Note 1 slip; EIHL Roster/DOPS, EIH and IHUK policy documents not on disk.

**scripts/ (coordinator, between waves):** `check_layer_echo` swallows the Sources trailer into KT; `check_caption_echo` compares against the whole block; `md_to_speech.py` CAN/CSA, "engine" field at :3863, U12 casing, "Fourth, If", "hockey 101"; unknown-flag handling (two exit 0, two traceback); `check_callout_flow.classify()` keys on how a line opens; `check_pointers` hardcodes the content root; `check_facts --near` misses blocks at 14; `check_quote_drift` has no unflattened pass; synthesize_style_b lacks a `--doc-id` sidecar; the podcast upload check reads local, not the deployed feed. About ten requested tools were never built (listed in parts B/E/H).

**Owner decisions (collected):** IIHF 48.1 vs the "keep Rule 43" ruling; HC goalie hit under 7.5 (shooting :536); Wave 34 questions 2–6 (In-House reach to Scotland/NI, CARHA suspensions, USAH Level 1, caption trim, centre-fly label); the "What it costs" frame; Google Analytics (cloudfront.tf:120 comment contradicts production); In-short above facts in about 300 sections; HC 8.6 vs 8.7 puck-first slide; KT count ceiling; tactics-ratio target; `## Check yourself` in 36 documents; PWHL as standing sixth book and `Rule set:` headers; PNIHL/NIHL trailer; the five D-zone families; centre-low-zone-collapse exception; dead `creaseThePuckDecides` spec; panels per page; "(2) minor" shorthand; `*.local.md` in VCS; podcast ear test.

## What this method could not have found

Every check was lexical: I searched for the phrase each row quoted, or for its rule number. Where that text is gone I classed the row DONE. But a defect reworded during R1/R2, or moved to another layer, would pass that test while still being there. This is the round-73 "qualifier changes jobs" shape and the paraphrase-survival shape the plan itself warns about. Several "DONE" results rest on an absence: the phrase is gone, and I did not read the replacement for meaning. Examples are the IIHF non-reckless rows, "can call either", and switching (c)/(f). I did not verify any rule against the rulebooks, so where a row's claim is "now carried", that means the text is present, not that it is correct. Rows that cite only bare line numbers (goaltender LEFT rows, bc :667/:669, defender CM :816/KT :893) could not be mapped after the rewrites. The helmet-grab and boards-posture "sweeps" need paragraph-level reading that a per-line scan cannot do. Finally, I did not render anything through md_to_speech, so no chunk-distance or voiced-alone judgement was made, and the "minor/female misheard" class is listed only by string match.

No escalation-gap tool exists.

## What this method could not have found

Every check here was lexical: find the cited phrase and see whether it survives. That catches removed or rewritten sentences. It cannot catch a defect that left no unusual string behind. Examples are a "four books" or "every book" claim reworded into a different count word, a summary layer still built on an answer the body has since corrected, or a claim that moved layers and lost what it qualified. I did not verify any rule against a rulebook. So the DONE rows mean only that the described wording is gone or changed. They do not mean the replacement is true. The harsher/permissive DONE verdicts (W37 c/e, W36 every-book ceilings) rest on a few sample carriers, not a whole-document read. The DTR replacement at defending_the_rush.md:1041 ("ends your game in every book here" for a forceful hit that stays off the head) was not tested against IIHF 43's reckless-only tier, which is exactly the W35 Q1 open question. The UNCLEAR sweep rows (PWHL short shelf, empty-net split, unchanged summary layers, caption hosts, NN7) need a reader per document.

## What this method could not have found

The checks were greps for each row's own words and nearby keywords, plus closure records taken on trust. So:

- **Rewritten wording**: a defect that survives in different words would pass, for example a 32(d) flat price that does not say "one-game", or a 53(a) gloss phrased without "anywhere".
- **Rulebook-side completeness**: nothing was checked against the rulebooks, so whether a cited rule is the book's whole answer was never tested. That covers rows 682, 692 and 695 entirely.
- **Closures taken on trust**: many rows were classed DONE because W32/W33 recorded every listed document as closed, without re-reading those documents. A closure that did not reach every layer (a Key Takeaway or facts line voiced alone) would be invisible here.
- **Captions**: the diagram-caption check for row 690 was a regex heuristic, not a read of every caption.

## What this method could not have found

These rows were triaged by grepping the row's own words and reading the W32/W33 per-file annotations. So a row whose cited sentence was rewritten into different words scores DONE even if the underlying claim survives elsewhere in the same document, or in another layer, in a new phrasing. That is the "brief a claim, never a line" failure. Where I accepted a W32/W33 "closed" (700, 701, 727, 751, 753, 763), I did not re-test the claim. That includes the CARHA/PWHL roughing-route sweep for a kick at a minor, which may never have been run even though the files are marked closed. 742 shows that a "closed" annotation can coexist with the original string. No rule was checked against a rulebook. No audio was rendered, so the chunk-distance and voiced-alone questions behind 714, 758 and 760 are untested. The 34-header count in 759 is a lexical match, not a judgement that each header is wrong.

## What this method could not have found

This was grep-for-the-cited-words triage. A row marked DONE means the words it cited are gone or changed. It does not mean the replacement text is correct, and per CLAUDE.md a rewrite is new, unreviewed text. In body_contact_and_battles.md in particular, every cited phrase has vanished in rewrites, so the original defects could have survived under new wording that no grep for the old strings would find. That is the row 807 "no lexical trace" problem in a new form. No rule was checked against a rulebook. The visual and diagram rows (820, 827–829, 839 collisions) need a browser render, which this method did not do. Several "named docs closed" DONEs (809, 811, 812) rely on the plan's own W32/W33 annotations plus spot greps, not on re-reading the review records. Cross-document sweeps that the rows describe as "siblings unswept" were only sampled.

## What this method could not have found

Items 2–6 were classed from records and aggregate `check_readability` columns, not by reading sections. Whether each section leads with the instruction, uses plain words, or keeps every safety limb was not tested. Rows closed "by S1" ((k), (n)) were trusted to the record, not re-grepped claim by claim. No rule was checked against a rulebook, so a DONE means the described text changed, not that the new text is right. GitHub's dependabot count and the live production page were not re-checked. R1 (c) could not be tested because the row does not say what the EIHL collision is.

## What this method could not have found

Every check here was a length or count measurement or a phrase grep, so a row closes as DONE whenever the cited words or sizes are gone. That cannot show whether the rewrite that removed them kept the limb they carried, or whether new units now carry the same problem in different words. A safety qualifier dropped in R1/R2 shortening, a garden path rewritten into a new garden path, or a 300-char KT that still leads with a tariff would all pass. Three things were never tested: the roman-numeral and "voiced alone" items, which need an `md_to_speech` render; the restatement-ladder and body-re-aim rows (980, 982), which need per-claim layer tests; and the W32/W33 "closed"/"wontfix" labels, which were accepted from the plan text without reading the review records.

## What this method could not have found

Every check here was lexical or mechanical: grep over the working tree, a de-tagged SSML grep, and counts over a `site/dist` that may be stale. Nothing was viewed in a browser, so every rendering row (1113, 1115, 1116, 1121, contrast, wrapping, phone width) is UNCLEAR rather than tested. The working tree was being edited by concurrent work while I read it, so a DONE can be undone and a file:line can drift.

A DONE that rests on "phrase gone" is the weak lexical closure that row 1127 itself warns about. The defect may have moved into a reworded sentence that no longer matches the search. The pronoun (1037) and punctuation (1047) rows especially need a reader per rendered chunk, not a grep.

Podcast DONEs are inferred from plan text and file presence, not from listening or re-reviewing the 39 scripts. In particular I did not check whether the remade scripts reintroduced any of the specific defects. The defender and skating scripts were checked for only the quoted phrases.

I verified no rules against rulebooks, by brief.

**ORDER OF WORK (owner, 19 Oct 2026: "lets focus on readability changes first" … "the order you presented above is a good order"):** (1) P1 readability, including SHORTER IS BETTER; (2) ⭐ Priority 1, how to play; (3) tooling; (4) quotations, citations and provenance; (5) captions and diagrams; (6) British scope; (7) book counts and scope promises; (8) the remaining rule-specific groups. The owner approved the page design from the local Shooting preview: "looks good lets follow that for all pages".** **Owner decisions of 16 Oct 2026 are recorded in `project/podcast_two_formats.md`, which is the spec.** Work order:
  1. Site refactor with no change to output. Diff the feed, playlists and pages against main.
  2. Renumber the live Conversation feed to section seasons, with section-prefixed titles and synthetic dates. Every guid must stay unchanged, enforced by a scripted assertion.

  ⚠️ **Must be settled before any re-made Conversation episode is published.** The owner is re-making all 39 Conversation episodes. `build_podcast_audio.py` keeps each existing audio path, and CloudFront caches `/audio/*` for a year, so re-uploading to the same key would keep serving the old audio. Re-made Conversation masters therefore need a new, versioned or hashed enclosure path, as Playing principles already uses. The guid stays the same, which is allowed: an enclosure URL may change, a guid may not. This needs a `--move-audio-path` policy, or a hash in the name, and the same change in `check-podcast-guids.mjs`, which currently pins the enclosure URLs.

### Wave 36 follow-ups (15 Oct)

Wave 36 applied the two owner rulings of 15 Oct (Hockey Canada head contact priced via 7.6/7.7; CARHA relabelled to the 2026 book) across 34 documents, then four reviewers and a fix round; see [`wave36_2026-10-15.md`](../reviews/wave36_2026-10-15.md).

Every OPEN row from the six triages, merged and de-duplicated across slices. Format: `- site — defect/task
(direction) — (plan line N, the OLD plan = the archive) — [which triage; merge note; test state]`. Content line
numbers are as the plan or the triage recorded them and **drift** — re-locate by content before editing. Within each
family, permissive rows (a reader told an act is cheaper than it is) come first. **"untested since 30 Sep 2026"**
means the triage could not confirm the row is still live; it stays open until someone looks. Tags `T1`–`T6` name the
triage slice (T3a–T3d are the four parts of slice 3); `merged across` means the same defect was recorded in several
places in the old plan.

### P1 readability — tactics ratio, layers, kernels, lengths

- **[READABILITY PROGRAMME, owner-approved 19 Oct 2026: HIGHEST PRIORITY]** The owner approved this list ("yes please … put these in the readability section so they are the highest priority"). Per-document passes apply items 1–6. Item 7 is coordinator tooling. Items 8–11 need owner sign-off on the site design.
  1. **Short paragraphs:** at most about 4–5 sentences and about 1,200 characters each. Split wherever the idea changes. Some paragraphs today run past 9,000 characters.
  2. **Rules go last in each section:** lead with what to do, then one short "What it costs" block (or the existing facts block) at the end. Every safety limb stays in that section.
  3. **Each layer does a different job:** Key focus = the 5–10 things a player does; Overview = why they matter; Key Takeaways = one line per instruction; Common Mistakes = the mistake, then the fix. No sentence repeated word for word across layers.
  4. **Plain words over rulebook words in the teaching text:** "thrown out of the game", not "game misconduct". Define each term once. Facts lines and costs blocks may use the exact terms.
  5. **Fewer rule numbers in prose:** keep them in facts lines, costs blocks and the Sources trailer; elsewhere name the book.
  6. **Fewer ⚠️ markers:** keep them only for real injury, ejection or penalty risk. Use check_marker_pairs, 0 lost; a paragraph that had a marker keeps at least one.
  7. **A readability measure (tooling):** a script reporting per-document word count, mean and maximum sentence length, maximum paragraph length, hard-word rate and Key Takeaway lengths, so each round shows its progress.
  8. **Consistent terms across documents** ("defender" vs "defenceman", "net" vs "goal frame"): pick one, glossary first, then sweep.
  9. **(Site, owner sign-off)** A "Just the essentials" view: each page opens with Key focus and Key Takeaways, and the full detail is one click away.
  10. **(Site, owner sign-off)** A one-line "in short" summary at the top of each long section.
  11. **(Site, owner sign-off)** Reading time per page. Also trim or collapse the long Sources trailers on the site.
  - **⚠️ R1 cross-document risk (raised by the DZC agent):** several R1 cuts replace a rule ladder with a pointer to another document (rules_primer, body_contact, special_teams, goaltender, defending_the_rush) while those documents are being cut concurrently. Before committing R1, a cross-document check must confirm that every pointed-at claim still exists at its target, and that each page still voices the safety limbs its own reader needs.
  - **R1 DONE (19 Oct).** All 39 documents have been shortened and reviewed. The record is in project/reviews/readability_r1_2026-10-19.md. Prose went from 1,068,411 to 794,955 words. Next is R2: apply programme items 1–6 to the first 20 documents, which had the v1 brief only.

    - **Coordinator errors R2 found, so do not repeat them in briefs.**
      - (1) My claim (h) said a position that "impairs the goalie's ability to move or defend" can cost the goal from outside the crease. That is wrong: NHL, IIHF and PWHL Table 14 Situations 5C and 5E say "Goal is allowed". Only contact, or stopping him moving freely in his crease, costs it. Blocking his way back is a 69.4 penalty and does not cost the goal, except under USAH Casebook 625 Situation 9.
      - (2) I suggested "head first or on his head, the harsher of 7.5 and head contact". Under the owner's ruling, harsher-of applies only to a head-first drive into the boards or goal frame.
      - (3) I suggested "(a bench minor under CARHA)" for a deliberate puck out of the rink. That is false, because CARHA 75(b) is a personal minor. Two agents refused it.

- **[OWNER, 19 Oct 2026: SHORTER IS BETTER]** Verbatim: *"make the text as short as possible, I want it to still be clear and easy to understand but shorter text is better then longer text as it is easier and faster to read"*. This is a standing rule for every edit and a readability workstream of its own. Cut words that do no work: repeated qualifiers, restated citations, book tours, throat-clearing, and a second sentence that says the first again. Prefer one short sentence to a long one. Keep every safety limb and honest disclosure (non-negotiables 3 and 4), but say each once and briefly. Rules detail belongs in the body or facts, not repeated in every layer. Measure each document's word count before and after, and review every cut for a lost limb.
